Is it... is it okay that I don't do those things?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you eat a lot, do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control what you're eating?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if I'd say I completely lose control, you know? It's not like I don't know what I'm doing. But... well, I guess sometimes, once I start, it does feel a bit like a switch gets flipped. \n\nI'll be sitting there, and I'll even be telling myself, \"Roy, just shut the bag and go inside,\" but... my hands just sort of keep reaching for more anyway. It's a little embarrassing to admit, actually. I should have better willpower at 54, right? \n\nI hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. Does that make sense, though? Is that what you meant by not being able to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're alone, or do you sometimes eat large amounts like this in front of others too?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I definitely try to keep it to myself, mostly. I mean, my wife, she's wonderful, but I wouldn't want her to see me putting away all that food. It's... it's a bit much, you know? \n\nSo, um, sometimes at dinner with her, I'll just have a small portion and act like I'm not really that hungry. And then... well, then later, I might take my takeout out to the truck, or I'll just sort of wait until she's busy with something else or gone to bed before I get into the leftovers in the garage. \n\nI just... I'd be pretty embarrassed if she saw how much I was actually eating. It's better if I just handle it on my own, right? I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is it pretty common for folks to want to keep that sort of thing private?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed or embarrassed about how much you eat, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah. Maybe even a bit disgusted with myself when it's all over. \n\nI've always thought of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, you know? A dependable guy who goes to work, does his shift, and handles his business. So when I'm out there sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. It's definitely not my best quality, and I want to be better... but is it pretty normal for people to get down on themselves like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually end up eating this way after a stressful day at work, or when you're feeling down, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Well, to be honest... I haven't really noticed a connection like that. \n\nI mean, work on the production line can be tiring, sure, but I wouldn't say I come home feeling anxious or upset. I try to just leave work at work, you know? I like to think I'm a pretty steady guy. So... no, it doesn't really seem to happen just because I've had a bad day. Sometimes it's a perfectly good day, and I still just... end up out there eating. \n\nI wish I could give you a better reason, because I feel like being stressed would at least make it make more sense. I hope it's okay that I don't really have a good excuse for it. Is it normal for this to happen even when someone isn't feeling down?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I try to be mindful, but... when I'm out in the truck or, you know, out in the workshop with the leftovers... it does go pretty quick.

Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I mean, I don't set out to gorge myself or anything, I really do try to keep it reasonable. But... if I'm being completely honest with you... yeah.

Well, um... it's usually pretty quick, I suppose. I mean, I don't stretch it out over the whole evening or anything like that. 

Usually, it's just... well, after a late shift, I might stop and grab a little takeout on the way home. I try to just leave work at work, you know? I like to think I'm a pretty steady guy. So... no, it doesn't really seem to happen just because I've had a bad day. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know?\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of overeating, do you ever try to compensate by making yourself sick, exercising a lot, or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I've never done anything like that. I mean, I know some folks do, but I wouldn't... I just couldn't make myself sick on purpose. And at my age, after a long shift on the floor, I'm definitely not hitting the gym or anything crazy. I just... I usually just feel a bit too full and go to sleep. \n\nI hope that's the right answer. I just wanted to be honest with you about it. Is it... is it okay that I don't do those things?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you eat a lot, do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control what you're eating?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if I'd say I completely lose control, you know? It's not like I don't know what I'm doing. But... well, I guess sometimes, once I start, it does feel a bit like a switch gets flipped. \n\nI'll be sitting there, and I'll even be telling myself, \"Roy, just shut the bag and go inside,\" but... my hands just sort of keep reaching for more anyway. It's a little embarrassing to admit, actually. I should have better willpower at 54, right? \n\nI hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. Does that make sense, though? Is that what you meant by not being able to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're alone, or do you sometimes eat large amounts like this in front of others too?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I definitely try to keep it to myself, mostly. I mean, my wife, she's wonderful, but I wouldn't want her to see me putting away all that food. It's... it's a bit much, you know? \n\nSo, um, sometimes at dinner with her, I'll just have a small portion and act like I'm not really that hungry. And then... well, then later, I might take my takeout out to the truck, or I'll just sort of wait until she's busy with something else or gone to bed before I get into the leftovers in the garage. \n\nI just... I'd be pretty embarrassed if she saw how much I was actually eating. It's better if I just handle it on my own, right? I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is it pretty common for folks to want to keep that sort of thing private?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed or embarrassed about how much you eat, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah. I do. I mean, I try not to be too hard on myself, but... it's hard not to feel a little, I don't know, ashamed. Maybe even a bit disgusted with myself when it's all over. \n\nI've always thought of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, you know? A dependable guy who goes to work, does his shift, and handles his business. So when I'm out there sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. It's definitely not my best quality, and I want to be better... but is it pretty normal for people to get down on themselves like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually end up eating this way after a stressful day at work, or when you're feeling down, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Well, to be honest... I haven't really noticed a connection like that. \n\nI mean, work on the production line can be tiring, sure, but I wouldn't say I come home feeling anxious or upset. I try to just leave work at work, you know? I like to think I'm a pretty steady guy. So... no, it doesn't really seem to happen just because I've had a bad day. Sometimes it's a perfectly good day, and I still just... end up out there eating. \n\nI wish I could give you a better reason, because I feel like being stressed would at least make it make more sense. I hope it's okay that I don't really have a good excuse for it. Is it normal for this to happen even when someone isn't feeling down?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger meals or eating sessions, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I try to be mindful, but... when I'm out in the truck or, you know, out in the workshop with the leftovers... it does go pretty quick. \n\nI don't really mean to, but I think I just... I eat so fast that half the time I barely even taste it. It's almost like I'm just trying to get it all down before my wife comes out and catches me, you know? I just want to get it over with so I can clean up the wrappers and get back inside. \n\nI know I should probably slow down and chew my food properly, like they always tell you. I really am trying to be better about that. Do a lot of people struggle with eating too fast when they're, um, eating privately like that?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you're eating a lot, do you usually keep eating until you feel uncomfortably full, like you've had more than you really should have?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I mean, I don't set out to gorge myself or anything, I really do try to keep it reasonable. But... if I'm being completely honest with you... yeah. \n\nBy the time I finally stop, it gets pretty uncomfortable. My stomach will be pulling really tight against my work pants, you know? And I just end up sitting there, leaned back, feeling uncomfortable enough that I really don't want to move for a little while. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it get to that point, and I promise I'm really trying to be better about listening to my body and knowing when to quit. I hope you don't think I'm just completely out of control for letting that happen. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from other folks?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger eating sessions, do you usually eat all that food within a short amount of time, like within a couple of hours, or does it stretch out over a longer part of the evening?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's usually pretty quick, I suppose. I mean, I don't stretch it out over the whole evening or anything like that. \n\nUsually, it's just... well, after a late shift, I might stop and grab a little takeout on the way home. Just, you know, maybe two burgers, some fries, a shake... just to take the edge off the hunger from working. But then... I don't know why, but I'll get home and still end up out in the garage with whatever leftovers I can find, almost like I'm having a whole second supper right on the heels of the first one. \n\nIt all happens pretty fast, probably within an hour or two at most. I know it sounds like a lot of food all at once, and I really am trying to cut back on those late-night stops. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these larger eating sessions happen? Like, would you say it's several times a week, once a week, or less often?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on the week, really. I try my best to keep it under control, I really do. But... if I'm being totally honest, for the past few months, it's been happening a bit more than I'd like. \n\nUsually, there's at least one night a week where I end up out there with the food. And, well... sometimes, if it's been a really rough day on the line, or if I've had a string of late shifts, it might happen two or three times in a week. \n\nI know that sounds like a lot, and I'm really trying to find better ways to unwind after work so I don't rely on the food so much. I hope you don't think too badly of me for letting it get to that point. Is that... is that the kind of frequency you usually see with this sort of thing?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'll end up having, you know, maybe two burgers, fries, a shake... which I know is a bit much for a guy my age. But then I'll still go home and sort of... clean out whatever leftovers we have in the garage fridge. It's almost like a second supper, I guess. \n\nI feel a little silly even bringing it up, to be honest. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know?\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of overeating, do you ever try to compensate by making yourself sick, exercising a lot, or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I've never done anything like that. I mean, I know some folks do, but I wouldn't... I just couldn't make myself sick on purpose. And at my age, after a long shift on the floor, I'm definitely not hitting the gym or anything crazy. I just... I usually just feel a bit too full and go to sleep. \n\nI hope that's the right answer. I just wanted to be honest with you about it. Is it... is it okay that I don't do those things?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you eat a lot, do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control what you're eating?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if I'd say I completely lose control, you know? It's not like I don't know what I'm doing. But... well, I guess sometimes, once I start, it does feel a bit like a switch gets flipped. \n\nI'll be sitting there, and I'll even be telling myself, \"Roy, just shut the bag and go inside,\" but... my hands just sort of keep reaching for more anyway. It's a little embarrassing to admit, actually. I should have better willpower at 54, right? \n\nI hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. Does that make sense, though? Is that what you meant by not being able to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're alone, or do you sometimes eat large amounts like this in front of others too?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I definitely try to keep it to myself, mostly. I mean, my wife, she's wonderful, but I wouldn't want her to see me putting away all that food. It's... it's a bit much, you know? \n\nSo, um, sometimes at dinner with her, I'll just have a small portion and act like I'm not really that hungry. And then... well, then later, I might take my takeout out to the truck, or I'll just sort of wait until she's busy with something else or gone to bed before I get into the leftovers in the garage. \n\nI just... I'd be pretty embarrassed if she saw how much I was actually eating. It's better if I just handle it on my own, right? I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is it pretty common for folks to want to keep that sort of thing private?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed or embarrassed about how much you eat, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah. I do. I mean, I try not to be too hard on myself, but... it's hard not to feel a little, I don't know, ashamed. Maybe even a bit disgusted with myself when it's all over. \n\nI've always thought of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, you know? A dependable guy who goes to work, does his shift, and handles his business. So when I'm out there sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. It's definitely not my best quality, and I want to be better... but is it pretty normal for people to get down on themselves like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually end up eating this way after a stressful day at work, or when you're feeling down, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Well, to be honest... I haven't really noticed a connection like that. \n\nI mean, work on the production line can be tiring, sure, but I wouldn't say I come home feeling anxious or upset. I try to just leave work at work, you know? I like to think I'm a pretty steady guy. So... no, it doesn't really seem to happen just because I've had a bad day. Sometimes it's a perfectly good day, and I still just... end up out there eating. \n\nI wish I could give you a better reason, because I feel like being stressed would at least make it make more sense. I hope it's okay that I don't really have a good excuse for it. Is it normal for this to happen even when someone isn't feeling down?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger meals or eating sessions, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I try to be mindful, but... when I'm out in the truck or, you know, out in the workshop with the leftovers... it does go pretty quick. \n\nI don't really mean to, but I think I just... I eat so fast that half the time I barely even taste it. It's almost like I'm just trying to get it all down before my wife comes out and catches me, you know? I just want to get it over with so I can clean up the wrappers and get back inside. \n\nI know I should probably slow down and chew my food properly, like they always tell you. I really am trying to be better about that. Do a lot of people struggle with eating too fast when they're, um, eating privately like that?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you're eating a lot, do you usually keep eating until you feel uncomfortably full, like you've had more than you really should have?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I mean, I don't set out to gorge myself or anything, I really do try to keep it reasonable. But... if I'm being completely honest with you... yeah. \n\nBy the time I finally stop, it gets pretty uncomfortable. My stomach will be pulling really tight against my work pants, you know? And I just end up sitting there, leaned back, feeling uncomfortable enough that I really don't want to move for a little while. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it get to that point, and I promise I'm really trying to be better about listening to my body and knowing when to quit. I hope you don't think I'm just completely out of control for letting that happen. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from other folks?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger eating sessions, do you usually eat all that food within a short amount of time, like within a couple of hours, or does it stretch out over a longer part of the evening?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's usually pretty quick, I suppose. I mean, I don't stretch it out over the whole evening or anything like that. \n\nUsually, it's just... well, after a late shift, I might stop and grab a little takeout on the way home. Just, you know, maybe two burgers, some fries, a shake... just to take the edge off the hunger from working. But then... I don't know why, but I'll get home and still end up out in the garage with whatever leftovers I can find, almost like I'm having a whole second supper right on the heels of the first one. \n\nIt all happens pretty fast, probably within an hour or two at most. I know it sounds like a lot of food all at once, and I really am trying to cut back on those late-night stops. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these larger eating sessions happen? Like, would you say it's several times a week, once a week, or less often?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on the week, really. I try my best to keep it under control, I really do. But... if I'm being totally honest, for the past few months, it's been happening a bit more than I'd like. \n\nUsually, there's at least one night a week where I end up out there with the food. And, well... sometimes, if it's been a really rough day on the line, or if I've had a string of late shifts, it might happen two or three times in a week. \n\nI know that sounds like a lot, and I'm really trying to find better ways to unwind after work so I don't rely on the food so much. I hope you don't think too badly of me for letting it get to that point. Is that... is that the kind of frequency you usually see with this sort of thing?\n\nDoctor: Do these eating sessions usually leave you feeling really upset, anxious, or distressed afterward?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try not to let it get to me too much, I really do. But... yeah. If I'm being completely honest, it does bother me quite a bit. \n\nI just... I feel pretty ashamed afterward. Kind of disgusted with myself, really. I mean, I've always thought of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, you know? People know they can depend on me at work and that I'll get the job done. So... ending up out in the garage, sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I'm going to work on having a better attitude about it. I hope I'm not sounding too pathetic saying all this... does that make sense to you, why it would bother me like that?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there have been times when you felt like you completely lost control while eating—like you couldn't stop eating even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't want you to think I just completely lose my mind or anything. I try really hard to keep a lid on it. But... yeah, I guess sometimes it does feel a bit like that. \n\nIt's almost like... well, once I start, it feels like a switch is flipped. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'Roy, shut the bag and go inside.' I know I should stop, I really do. But... my hands just keep reaching for more, almost like they're moving on their own. \n\nI know a grown man should have better willpower than that, and I promise I'm going to work on being stronger when that happens. I hope I'm not making myself sound too out of control... does that answer your question okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I hope you don't think too badly of me for letting it get to that point. I try not to let it get to me too much, I really do. But... yeah. If I'm being completely honest, it does bother me quite a bit. \n\nI just... I feel pretty ashamed afterward. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm going to crazy extremes or anything. I try to just be a good husband and keep things peaceful at home. But... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do keep it to myself. 

Like, when my wife and I sit down for dinner, I'll kind of... I'll act like I'm just not that hungry. I'll eat a little bit and say I had a big lunch at the plant. But then later on, I'll, uh... I'll head out to my truck, or I'll just wait until she's busy in another room or asleep. 

I just... I'd be so embarrassed for her to actually see how much I'm putting away during those times. She's a wonderful woman, and I really don't want her to see me like that or think less of me. I know I shouldn't be sneaking around behind her back, and I promise I'm going to try to be better about it. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible guy for hiding it from her... is that pretty common, keeping it a secret from family like that?

I just want to get it over with so I can clean up the wrappers and get back inside. \n\nI know I should probably slow down and chew my food properly, like they always tell you. I really am trying to be better about that. Well, um... I really try not to let it get in the way of the important things. I mean, I'm still making it to the plant every day, getting my shifts done, and I haven't missed a Sunday at church. I try to stay on top of all my responsibilities, I really do.

I haven't really noticed a connection like that. \n\nI mean, work on the production line can be tiring, sure, but I wouldn't say I come home feeling anxious or upset. Well, um... I really try to mind my manners, you know? I was raised to chew my food and take my time. But... yeah, I guess when I'm out in my truck or hiding out in the workshop, I do tend to rush through it a bit. 

It's not that I'm trying to be gluttonous or anything like that. It's just... I get so worried that my wife might come out looking for me, or that someone's going to catch me. So I end up eating so fast that... well, half the time I barely even taste what I'm eating. I'm just trying to get it down before anyone sees.

I know I should slow down and appreciate my food, and I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful about that. I promise I'm not usually a messy or rushed eater when I'm at the dinner table. I hope you don't think I'm completely lacking in self-control... is it pretty normal for people to eat faster when they're worried about being caught?

But then I'll still go home and sort of... clean out whatever leftovers we have in the garage fridge. It's almost like a second supper, I guess. \n\nI feel a little silly even bringing it up, to be honest. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know?\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of overeating, do you ever try to compensate by making yourself sick, exercising a lot, or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I've never done anything like that. I mean, I know some folks do, but I wouldn't... I just couldn't make myself sick on purpose. And at my age, after a long shift on the floor, I'm definitely not hitting the gym or anything crazy. I just... I usually just feel a bit too full and go to sleep. \n\nI hope that's the right answer. I just wanted to be honest with you about it. Is it... is it okay that I don't do those things?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you eat a lot, do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control what you're eating?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if I'd say I completely lose control, you know? It's not like I don't know what I'm doing. But... well, I guess sometimes, once I start, it does feel a bit like a switch gets flipped. \n\nI'll be sitting there, and I'll even be telling myself, \"Roy, just shut the bag and go inside,\" but... my hands just sort of keep reaching for more anyway. It's a little embarrassing to admit, actually. I should have better willpower at 54, right? \n\nI hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. Does that make sense, though? Is that what you meant by not being able to stop?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're alone, or do you sometimes eat large amounts like this in front of others too?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I definitely try to keep it to myself, mostly. I mean, my wife, she's wonderful, but I wouldn't want her to see me putting away all that food. It's... it's a bit much, you know? \n\nSo, um, sometimes at dinner with her, I'll just have a small portion and act like I'm not really that hungry. And then... well, then later, I might take my takeout out to the truck, or I'll just sort of wait until she's busy with something else or gone to bed before I get into the leftovers in the garage. \n\nI just... I'd be pretty embarrassed if she saw how much I was actually eating. It's better if I just handle it on my own, right? I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is it pretty common for folks to want to keep that sort of thing private?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed or embarrassed about how much you eat, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah. I do. I mean, I try not to be too hard on myself, but... it's hard not to feel a little, I don't know, ashamed. Maybe even a bit disgusted with myself when it's all over. \n\nI've always thought of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, you know? A dependable guy who goes to work, does his shift, and handles his business. So when I'm out there sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. It's definitely not my best quality, and I want to be better... but is it pretty normal for people to get down on themselves like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually end up eating this way after a stressful day at work, or when you're feeling down, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Well, to be honest... I haven't really noticed a connection like that. \n\nI mean, work on the production line can be tiring, sure, but I wouldn't say I come home feeling anxious or upset. I try to just leave work at work, you know? I like to think I'm a pretty steady guy. So... no, it doesn't really seem to happen just because I've had a bad day. Sometimes it's a perfectly good day, and I still just... end up out there eating. \n\nI wish I could give you a better reason, because I feel like being stressed would at least make it make more sense. I hope it's okay that I don't really have a good excuse for it. Is it normal for this to happen even when someone isn't feeling down?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger meals or eating sessions, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I try to be mindful, but... when I'm out in the truck or, you know, out in the workshop with the leftovers... it does go pretty quick. \n\nI don't really mean to, but I think I just... I eat so fast that half the time I barely even taste it. It's almost like I'm just trying to get it all down before my wife comes out and catches me, you know? I just want to get it over with so I can clean up the wrappers and get back inside. \n\nI know I should probably slow down and chew my food properly, like they always tell you. I really am trying to be better about that. Do a lot of people struggle with eating too fast when they're, um, eating privately like that?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you're eating a lot, do you usually keep eating until you feel uncomfortably full, like you've had more than you really should have?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I mean, I don't set out to gorge myself or anything, I really do try to keep it reasonable. But... if I'm being completely honest with you... yeah. \n\nBy the time I finally stop, it gets pretty uncomfortable. My stomach will be pulling really tight against my work pants, you know? And I just end up sitting there, leaned back, feeling uncomfortable enough that I really don't want to move for a little while. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it get to that point, and I promise I'm really trying to be better about listening to my body and knowing when to quit. I hope you don't think I'm just completely out of control for letting that happen. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from other folks?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger eating sessions, do you usually eat all that food within a short amount of time, like within a couple of hours, or does it stretch out over a longer part of the evening?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's usually pretty quick, I suppose. I mean, I don't stretch it out over the whole evening or anything like that. \n\nUsually, it's just... well, after a late shift, I might stop and grab a little takeout on the way home. Just, you know, maybe two burgers, some fries, a shake... just to take the edge off the hunger from working. But then... I don't know why, but I'll get home and still end up out in the garage with whatever leftovers I can find, almost like I'm having a whole second supper right on the heels of the first one. \n\nIt all happens pretty fast, probably within an hour or two at most. I know it sounds like a lot of food all at once, and I really am trying to cut back on those late-night stops. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these larger eating sessions happen? Like, would you say it's several times a week, once a week, or less often?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on the week, really. I try my best to keep it under control, I really do. But... if I'm being totally honest, for the past few months, it's been happening a bit more than I'd like. \n\nUsually, there's at least one night a week where I end up out there with the food. And, well... sometimes, if it's been a really rough day on the line, or if I've had a string of late shifts, it might happen two or three times in a week. \n\nI know that sounds like a lot, and I'm really trying to find better ways to unwind after work so I don't rely on the food so much. I hope you don't think too badly of me for letting it get to that point. Is that... is that the kind of frequency you usually see with this sort of thing?\n\nDoctor: Do these eating sessions usually leave you feeling really upset, anxious, or distressed afterward?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try not to let it get to me too much, I really do. But... yeah. If I'm being completely honest, it does bother me quite a bit. \n\nI just... I feel pretty ashamed afterward. Kind of disgusted with myself, really. I mean, I've always thought of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, you know? People know they can depend on me at work and that I'll get the job done. So... ending up out in the garage, sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I'm going to work on having a better attitude about it. I hope I'm not sounding too pathetic saying all this... does that make sense to you, why it would bother me like that?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there have been times when you felt like you completely lost control while eating—like you couldn't stop eating even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't want you to think I just completely lose my mind or anything. I try really hard to keep a lid on it. But... yeah, I guess sometimes it does feel a bit like that. \n\nIt's almost like... well, once I start, it feels like a switch is flipped. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'Roy, shut the bag and go inside.' I know I should stop, I really do. But... my hands just keep reaching for more, almost like they're moving on their own. \n\nI know a grown man should have better willpower than that, and I promise I'm going to work on being stronger when that happens. I hope I'm not making myself sound too out of control... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually go out of your way to hide these eating episodes from others, like making sure no one sees you eat that way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm going to crazy extremes or anything. I try to just be a good husband and keep things peaceful at home. But... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do keep it to myself. \n\nLike, when my wife and I sit down for dinner, I'll kind of... I'll act like I'm just not that hungry. I'll eat a little bit and say I had a big lunch at the plant. But then later on, I'll, uh... I'll head out to my truck, or I'll just wait until she's busy in another room or asleep. \n\nI just... I'd be so embarrassed for her to actually see how much I'm putting away during those times. She's a wonderful woman, and I really don't want her to see me like that or think less of me. I know I shouldn't be sneaking around behind her back, and I promise I'm going to try to be better about it. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible guy for hiding it from her... is that pretty common, keeping it a secret from family like that?\n\nDoctor: Has this eating pattern caused you a lot of trouble in your daily life, like affecting your relationships, work, or how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let it get in the way of the important things. I mean, I'm still making it to the plant every day, getting my shifts done, and I haven't missed a Sunday at church. I try to stay on top of all my responsibilities, I really do. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm looking at it honestly... it has been affecting things at home a little bit. Not in a big, dramatic way! Just... well, I've been a little more quiet. A bit withdrawn, I suppose. \n\nIt's just that... it takes a lot of energy, you know? Figuring out what excuse I'm going to use for why I'm not hungry for the dinner my wife made, or making sure I don't leave any wrappers in my truck where she might see them. I spend so much time thinking about how to cover my tracks that I end up just kind of keeping to myself so I don't slip up. \n\nI know I need to be more present for her, and I'm definitely going to work on being a better husband and not shutting her out. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm neglecting my family... does that make sense, how trying to hide it could make a guy act a little distant?\n\nDoctor: During these eating sessions, would you say you're eating significantly more rapidly than normal, like you're rushing through the food without really thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to mind my manners, you know? I was raised to chew my food and take my time. But... yeah, I guess when I'm out in my truck or hiding out in the workshop, I do tend to rush through it a bit. \n\nIt's not that I'm trying to be gluttonous or anything like that. It's just... I get so worried that my wife might come out looking for me, or that someone's going to catch me. So I end up eating so fast that... well, half the time I barely even taste what I'm eating. I'm just trying to get it down before anyone sees.\n\nI know I should slow down and appreciate my food, and I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful about that. I promise I'm not usually a messy or rushed eater when I'm at the dinner table. I hope you don't think I'm completely lacking in self-control... is it pretty normal for people to eat faster when they're worried about being caught?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really try to keep a handle on things, I do. I like to think of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, especially at the plant where you have to stay focused. But... well, when I'm out there in the truck with the food... it's a little different. 

I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely out of control or anything dramatic like that! But... I guess, if I'm being truthful, once I start eating, it feels like a switch just gets flipped. It's almost like my brain and my body aren't talking to each other. I'll literally be sitting there telling myself, "Roy, shut the bag, just wrap it up and go inside to your wife," but... my hands just keep reaching for more. 

I know I should just be able to put it down, and I'm really going to try harder to just stop when I tell myself to stop. I promise I'm not usually someone who just gives up like that. I hope you don't think I'm weak-willed or anything... is it common for people to feel like they can't stop even when their brain is telling them to?

I haven't really noticed a connection like that. \n\nI mean, work on the production line can be tiring, sure, but I wouldn't say I come home feeling anxious or upset. I eat so fast that half the time I barely even taste it. It's almost like I'm just trying to get it all down before my wife comes out and catches me, you know? I just want to get it over with so I can clean up the wrappers and get back inside. \n\nI know I should probably slow down and chew my food properly, like they always tell you. I really am trying to be better about that. Do a lot of people struggle with eating too fast when they're, um, eating privately like that?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you're eating a lot, do you usually keep eating until you feel uncomfortably full, like you've had more than you really should have?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I mean, I don't set out to gorge myself or anything, I really do try to keep it reasonable. But... if I'm being completely honest with you... yeah. \n\nBy the time I finally stop, it gets pretty uncomfortable. My stomach will be pulling really tight against my work pants, you know? And I just end up sitting there, leaned back, feeling uncomfortable enough that I really don't want to move for a little while. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it get to that point, and I promise I'm really trying to be better about listening to my body and knowing when to quit. I hope you don't think I'm just completely out of control for letting that happen. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from other folks?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger eating sessions, do you usually eat all that food within a short amount of time, like within a couple of hours, or does it stretch out over a longer part of the evening?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's usually pretty quick, I suppose. I mean, I don't stretch it out over the whole evening or anything like that. \n\nUsually, it's just... well, after a late shift, I might stop and grab a little takeout on the way home. I know it sounds like a lot of food all at once, and I really am trying to cut back on those late-night stops. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? Like, would you say it's several times a week, once a week, or less often?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on the week, really. I try my best to keep it under control, I really do. And, well... sometimes, if it's been a really rough day on the line, or if I've had a string of late shifts, it might happen two or three times in a week. \n\nI know that sounds like a lot, and I'm really trying to find better ways to unwind after work so I don't rely on the food so much. I hope you don't think too badly of me for letting it get to that point. So... ending up out in the garage, sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I'm going to work on having a better attitude about it. I don't want you to think I just completely lose my mind or anything. I try really hard to keep a lid on it. But... yeah, I guess sometimes it does feel a bit like that. \n\nIt's almost like... well, once I start, it feels like a switch is flipped. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'Roy, shut the bag and go inside.' I know I should stop, I really do. But... my hands just keep reaching for more, almost like they're moving on their own. \n\nI know a grown man should have better willpower than that, and I promise I'm going to work on being stronger when that happens. I hope I'm not making myself sound too out of control... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually go out of your way to hide these eating episodes from others, like making sure no one sees you eat that way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm going to crazy extremes or anything. I try to just be a good husband and keep things peaceful at home. But... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do keep it to myself. \n\nLike, when my wife and I sit down for dinner, I'll kind of... I'll act like I'm just not that hungry. I'll eat a little bit and say I had a big lunch at the plant. But then later on, I'll, uh... I'll head out to my truck, or I'll just wait until she's busy in another room or asleep. \n\nI just... I hope you don't think I'm a terrible guy for hiding it from her... is that pretty common, keeping it a secret from family like that?\n\nDoctor: Has this eating pattern caused you a lot of trouble in your daily life, like affecting your relationships, work, or how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let it get in the way of the important things. I mean, I'm still making it to the plant every day, getting my shifts done, and I haven't missed a Sunday at church. I try to stay on top of all my responsibilities, I really do. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm looking at it honestly... it has been affecting things at home a little bit. Not in a big, dramatic way! Figuring out what excuse I'm going to use for why I'm not hungry for the dinner my wife made, or making sure I don't leave any wrappers in my truck where she might see them. I spend so much time thinking about how to cover my tracks that I end up just kind of keeping to myself so I don't slip up. \n\nI know I need to be more present for her, and I'm definitely going to work on being a better husband and not shutting her out. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm neglecting my family... does that make sense, how trying to hide it could make a guy act a little distant?\n\nDoctor: During these eating sessions, would you say you're eating significantly more rapidly than normal, like you're rushing through the food without really thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to mind my manners, you know? I was raised to chew my food and take my time. But... yeah, I guess when I'm out in my truck or hiding out in the workshop, I do tend to rush through it a bit. \n\nIt's not that I'm trying to be gluttonous or anything like that. It's just... I'm just trying to get it down before anyone sees.\n\nI know I should slow down and appreciate my food, and I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful about that. I really try not to go too overboard. I don't want you to think I'm completely lacking any discipline or anything. I hope I'm not sounding too pathetic saying all this... does that make sense to you, why it would bother me like that?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there have been times when you felt like you completely lost control while eating—like you couldn't stop eating even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't want you to think I just completely lose my mind or anything. I try really hard to keep a lid on it. But... yeah, I guess sometimes it does feel a bit like that. \n\nIt's almost like... well, once I start, it feels like a switch is flipped. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'Roy, shut the bag and go inside.' I know I should stop, I really do. But... my hands just keep reaching for more, almost like they're moving on their own. \n\nI know a grown man should have better willpower than that, and I promise I'm going to work on being stronger when that happens. I hope I'm not making myself sound too out of control... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually go out of your way to hide these eating episodes from others, like making sure no one sees you eat that way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm going to crazy extremes or anything. I try to just be a good husband and keep things peaceful at home. But... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do keep it to myself. \n\nLike, when my wife and I sit down for dinner, I'll kind of... I'll act like I'm just not that hungry. I'll eat a little bit and say I had a big lunch at the plant. But then later on, I'll, uh... I'll head out to my truck, or I'll just wait until she's busy in another room or asleep. \n\nI just... I'd be so embarrassed for her to actually see how much I'm putting away during those times. She's a wonderful woman, and I really don't want her to see me like that or think less of me. I know I shouldn't be sneaking around behind her back, and I promise I'm going to try to be better about it. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible guy for hiding it from her... is that pretty common, keeping it a secret from family like that?\n\nDoctor: Has this eating pattern caused you a lot of trouble in your daily life, like affecting your relationships, work, or how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let it get in the way of the important things. I mean, I'm still making it to the plant every day, getting my shifts done, and I haven't missed a Sunday at church. I try to stay on top of all my responsibilities, I really do. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm looking at it honestly... it has been affecting things at home a little bit. Not in a big, dramatic way! Just... well, I've been a little more quiet. A bit withdrawn, I suppose. \n\nIt's just that... it takes a lot of energy, you know? Figuring out what excuse I'm going to use for why I'm not hungry for the dinner my wife made, or making sure I don't leave any wrappers in my truck where she might see them. I spend so much time thinking about how to cover my tracks that I end up just kind of keeping to myself so I don't slip up. \n\nI know I need to be more present for her, and I'm definitely going to work on being a better husband and not shutting her out. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm neglecting my family... does that make sense, how trying to hide it could make a guy act a little distant?\n\nDoctor: During these eating sessions, would you say you're eating significantly more rapidly than normal, like you're rushing through the food without really thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to mind my manners, you know? I was raised to chew my food and take my time. But... yeah, I guess when I'm out in my truck or hiding out in the workshop, I do tend to rush through it a bit. \n\nIt's not that I'm trying to be gluttonous or anything like that. It's just... I get so worried that my wife might come out looking for me, or that someone's going to catch me. So I end up eating so fast that... well, half the time I barely even taste what I'm eating. I'm just trying to get it down before anyone sees.\n\nI know I should slow down and appreciate my food, and I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful about that. I promise I'm not usually a messy or rushed eater when I'm at the dinner table. I hope you don't think I'm completely lacking in self-control... is it pretty normal for people to eat faster when they're worried about being caught?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger eating sessions, would you say that you're eating a much larger amount of food in one go than most people would eat in a similar time period under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to go too overboard. I don't want you to think I'm completely lacking any discipline or anything. But... yeah, I guess it is a pretty large amount, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nActually, that's kind of what finally made me realize I should come in and talk to someone. Like, after my late shifts at the plant, I'll stop for takeout on the way home. And I'll end up eating, you know, two whole burgers, fries, a shake... and then I'll still go home and clean out whatever leftovers we have out in the garage fridge. It's almost like I'm having a whole second supper. \n\nI know that's way more than a normal guy should be eating at that hour, and I promise I'm really going to work on cutting back and being healthier. I hope you don't think I'm just totally gluttonous for putting away that much food... does it make sense that a guy might overdo it a bit like that after a long, hard shift?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what goes through your mind during these eating sessions—specifically, do you feel like you're unable to stop eating even when you want to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... I know I just mentioned this a second ago, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much. I really want to be helpful and give you the right answers. But, yeah, like I was saying about that switch flipping... 

If I had to give you a specific time, it would probably be just last week. I had stopped on the way home, like I do, and I was sitting out in the truck. I had already finished the first burger, and I was starting on the fries. And I just kept thinking, "Roy, shut the bag. Just wrap it up, leave the rest, and go inside to your wife." I really, truly wanted to stop right then and there. I knew I should. 

But... it's like my hands weren't listening to me. I just kept reaching into the bag for more fries, and then I started on the second burger. It's not that I wanted to keep eating, I promise. It's just... I couldn't seem to make myself stop, even though my brain was telling me to. 

I'm definitely going to work on being stronger about that, though. I know I just need to try harder to listen to my own common sense. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying my best to explain it right... is it okay that I used that same example again?

Do a lot of people struggle with eating too fast when they're, um, eating privately like that?\n\nDoctor: During these times when you're eating a lot, do you usually keep eating until you feel uncomfortably full, like you've had more than you really should have?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do, yeah. I mean, I don't set out to gorge myself or anything, I really do try to keep it reasonable. But... if I'm being completely honest with you... yeah. \n\nBy the time I finally stop, it gets pretty uncomfortable. My stomach will be pulling really tight against my work pants, you know? And I just end up sitting there, leaned back, feeling uncomfortable enough that I really don't want to move for a little while. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it get to that point, and I promise I'm really trying to be better about listening to my body and knowing when to quit. I hope you don't think I'm just completely out of control for letting that happen. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from other folks?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger eating sessions, do you usually eat all that food within a short amount of time, like within a couple of hours, or does it stretch out over a longer part of the evening?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's usually pretty quick, I suppose. I mean, I don't stretch it out over the whole evening or anything like that. \n\nUsually, it's just... well, after a late shift, I might stop and grab a little takeout on the way home. Just, you know, maybe two burgers, some fries, a shake... just to take the edge off the hunger from working. But then... I don't know why, but I'll get home and still end up out in the garage with whatever leftovers I can find, almost like I'm having a whole second supper right on the heels of the first one. \n\nIt all happens pretty fast, probably within an hour or two at most. I know it sounds like a lot of food all at once, and I really am trying to cut back on those late-night stops. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these larger eating sessions happen? Like, would you say it's several times a week, once a week, or less often?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on the week, really. I try my best to keep it under control, I really do. But... if I'm being totally honest, for the past few months, it's been happening a bit more than I'd like. \n\nUsually, there's at least one night a week where I end up out there with the food. And, well... sometimes, if it's been a really rough day on the line, or if I've had a string of late shifts, it might happen two or three times in a week. \n\nI know that sounds like a lot, and I'm really trying to find better ways to unwind after work so I don't rely on the food so much. I hope you don't think too badly of me for letting it get to that point. Is that... is that the kind of frequency you usually see with this sort of thing?\n\nDoctor: Do these eating sessions usually leave you feeling really upset, anxious, or distressed afterward?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try not to let it get to me too much, I really do. But... yeah. If I'm being completely honest, it does bother me quite a bit. \n\nI just... I feel pretty ashamed afterward. Kind of disgusted with myself, really. I mean, I've always thought of myself as a pretty disciplined guy, you know? People know they can depend on me at work and that I'll get the job done. So... ending up out in the garage, sneaking around with food like that... it just makes me feel like I'm really letting myself down. \n\nI know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I'm going to work on having a better attitude about it. I hope I'm not sounding too pathetic saying all this... does that make sense to you, why it would bother me like that?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there have been times when you felt like you completely lost control while eating—like you couldn't stop eating even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't want you to think I just completely lose my mind or anything. I try really hard to keep a lid on it. But... yeah, I guess sometimes it does feel a bit like that. \n\nIt's almost like... well, once I start, it feels like a switch is flipped. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'Roy, shut the bag and go inside.' I know I should stop, I really do. But... my hands just keep reaching for more, almost like they're moving on their own. \n\nI know a grown man should have better willpower than that, and I promise I'm going to work on being stronger when that happens. I hope I'm not making myself sound too out of control... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually go out of your way to hide these eating episodes from others, like making sure no one sees you eat that way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm going to crazy extremes or anything. I try to just be a good husband and keep things peaceful at home. But... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do keep it to myself. \n\nLike, when my wife and I sit down for dinner, I'll kind of... I'll act like I'm just not that hungry. I'll eat a little bit and say I had a big lunch at the plant. But then later on, I'll, uh... I'll head out to my truck, or I'll just wait until she's busy in another room or asleep. \n\nI just... I'd be so embarrassed for her to actually see how much I'm putting away during those times. She's a wonderful woman, and I really don't want her to see me like that or think less of me. I know I shouldn't be sneaking around behind her back, and I promise I'm going to try to be better about it. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible guy for hiding it from her... is that pretty common, keeping it a secret from family like that?\n\nDoctor: Has this eating pattern caused you a lot of trouble in your daily life, like affecting your relationships, work, or how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to let it get in the way of the important things. I mean, I'm still making it to the plant every day, getting my shifts done, and I haven't missed a Sunday at church. I try to stay on top of all my responsibilities, I really do. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm looking at it honestly... it has been affecting things at home a little bit. Not in a big, dramatic way! Just... well, I've been a little more quiet. A bit withdrawn, I suppose. \n\nIt's just that... it takes a lot of energy, you know? Figuring out what excuse I'm going to use for why I'm not hungry for the dinner my wife made, or making sure I don't leave any wrappers in my truck where she might see them. I spend so much time thinking about how to cover my tracks that I end up just kind of keeping to myself so I don't slip up. \n\nI know I need to be more present for her, and I'm definitely going to work on being a better husband and not shutting her out. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm neglecting my family... does that make sense, how trying to hide it could make a guy act a little distant?\n\nDoctor: During these eating sessions, would you say you're eating significantly more rapidly than normal, like you're rushing through the food without really thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to mind my manners, you know? I was raised to chew my food and take my time. But... yeah, I guess when I'm out in my truck or hiding out in the workshop, I do tend to rush through it a bit. \n\nIt's not that I'm trying to be gluttonous or anything like that. It's just... I get so worried that my wife might come out looking for me, or that someone's going to catch me. So I end up eating so fast that... well, half the time I barely even taste what I'm eating. I'm just trying to get it down before anyone sees.\n\nI know I should slow down and appreciate my food, and I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful about that. I promise I'm not usually a messy or rushed eater when I'm at the dinner table. I hope you don't think I'm completely lacking in self-control... is it pretty normal for people to eat faster when they're worried about being caught?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these larger eating sessions, would you say that you're eating a much larger amount of food in one go than most people would eat in a similar time period under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to go too overboard. I don't want you to think I'm completely lacking any discipline or anything. But... yeah, I guess it is a pretty large amount, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nActually, that's kind of what finally made me realize I should come in and talk to someone. Like, after my late shifts at the plant, I'll stop for takeout on the way home. And I'll end up eating, you know, two whole burgers, fries, a shake... and then I'll still go home and clean out whatever leftovers we have out in the garage fridge. It's almost like I'm having a whole second supper. \n\nI know that's way more than a normal guy should be eating at that hour, and I promise I'm really going to work on cutting back and being healthier. I hope you don't think I'm just totally gluttonous for putting away that much food... does it make sense that a guy might overdo it a bit like that after a long, hard shift?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what goes through your mind during these eating sessions—specifically, do you feel like you're unable to stop eating even when you want to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'll literally be sitting there telling myself, \"Roy, shut the bag, just wrap it up and go inside to your wife,\" but... my hands just keep reaching for more. \n\nI know I should just be able to put it down, and I'm really going to try harder to just stop when I tell myself to stop.
